# Operate Plugins

Status: supported configuration and activation procedure; no general operator install or remove CLI exists
Audience: platform operator, security operator, pipeline operator
Owner: platform configuration owner
Evidence: skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/config/platform.schema.json; skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/registry/activation.ts; skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/packages/install.ts
Applies to: `pipeline-platform.v2` at the recorded source revision
Last verified: 2026-10-09 at source revision `c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0`; verification did not include live external plugin activation

## Purpose

Select, activate, observe, drain, disable, replace, or retire a plugin without
allowing project content to expand trust or permissions. This page is the only
canonical plugin-lifecycle procedure.

The generated [plugin catalogue](../extend/plugin-catalogue/README.md) is the
published, dynamic package list. Counts and package names are not copied here
because they change with the source tree.

## Complete Operator Views

The generated rows below assign every discovered package to an operator task,
host, role, surface, and declared dependency. They also assign every discovered
registration to its external-service evidence. Publication fails when discovery
and these rows drift. Use the [recursive plugin configuration
reference](../reference/plugin-configuration.md) to interpret nested fields and
conditional schema branches.

`source-backed unknown` is a stop condition when it affects authority, secrets,
data ownership, or a required live dependency. It is not evidence that no
dependency exists.

Accepted values: `boolean\|string with no narrower scalar constraint; The inline schema declares boolean\|string for plugin values. The runtime separately applies this normalization to the selected plugin or environment value: Passed unchanged by config resolution, then normalized as a boolean or trimmed lowercase text: 1, true, yes, on, required, enabled, isolated, network-policy, network_policy or documented marks isolation; other values are false`. Omission: No value default; an omitted value remains undefined. Registration configuration is merged first, then service configuration, then hook configuration. Later defined keys replace earlier keys; undefined keys are ignored. Null and empty values replace earlier values. The merged config.redisNetworkIsolation, when not undefined, wins over env.REDIS_NETWORK_ISOLATION; normalization then runs. Empty value: The declared inline type still applies to plugin values. If a null or empty value reaches the resolver, it overrides earlier configuration and the environment before normalization. Empty is passed unchanged to the Redis security-policy reader. Change effect: Changing `$.redisNetworkIsolation` changes this exact configured behavior when the OpenClaw-to-Redis host observer next loads it: Selects the network-isolation binding passed to the Redis connection used by the OpenClaw-to-Redis host observer. Failure: Required-value or numeric errors stop observer config resolution; Redis policy and connection failures can occur during activation.<br>The OpenClaw deployment owns sensitive configuration, process environment, credential projection, and rotation.<br>No credential is supplied to the diagnostic subscription. OpenClaw owns model credentials and diagnostic production; observer Redis password/TLS/isolation authority is documented separately and is not proven by SDK availability. Lists the exact URL origins that the bounded HTTP capability adapter can contact. Accepted values: `array with minItems=1, uniqueItems=true`. Omission: The schema declares no default. The immediate parent requires this field. Validation without default insertion rejects omission. Empty value: An empty array is invalid because minItems requires at least one item. Change effect: Changing `$.allowedOrigins` changes this exact configured behavior when the bounded HTTP capability adapter next loads it: Lists the exact URL origins that the bounded HTTP capability adapter can contact. Failure: Schema validation rejects `$.allowedOrigins` when it violates `array with minItems=1, uniqueItems=true`. The linked consumer at skills/common/plugins/network-http/src/adapter.ts:19 owns any later operational rejection for this field; no unrelated target, path, secret, or resource failure is inferred.<br>`allowedOrigins[]`: `type="string"`; `format="uri"`. Lists the exact URL origins that the bounded HTTP capability adapter can contact. Accepted values: `string with format="uri"`. Omission: The schema declares no default. [activate in skills/common/plugins/openclaw-agent-events/src/adapter.ts](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/1c0c780211a98fd2ebf9beffb1ace937fe079c04/skills/common/plugins/openclaw-agent-events/src/adapter.ts).<br>ready() requires sdk.on and registers every selected hook. Produce an authorized host event, then drain status and verify emitted/failed/rejected counts and the canonical namespaced event. Enforce maxQueueEvents/default 256, maxQueueBytes/default one MiB and drainTimeoutMs/default five seconds; shutdown unsubscribes before draining. Test both capacity rejection and cancellation. The lock holder owns only transient OS lock state; ending stdin releases the holder and retains store data. [skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/observability/durable-delivery.ts:185-201](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/1c0c780211a98fd2ebf9beffb1ace937fe079c04/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/observability/durable-delivery.ts#L185-L201), [skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/observability/durable-delivery.ts:221-230](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/1c0c780211a98fd2ebf9beffb1ace937fe079c04/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/observability/durable-delivery.ts#L221-L230). | No direct network preflight for this invocation. Verify local store and artifact/capability access using the registration's contract. [skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/observability/durable-delivery.ts:188](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/1c0c780211a98fd2ebf9beffb1ace937fe079c04/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/observability/durable-delivery.ts#L188-L188), [skills/nova/plugins/remote-test-gate/src/evidence-adapter.ts:22](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/1c0c780211a98fd2ebf9beffb1ace937fe079c04/skills/nova/plugins/remote-test-gate/src/evidence-adapter.ts#L22-L22).<br>Before using a store, check that /usr/bin/flock supports GNU --exclusive --timeout, and /bin/sh and /bin/cat are executable. A BusyBox symlink or binary existence does not prove compatibility. On an isolated writable store, verify bounded lock acquisition, a durable write/read, contention failure after the five-second lock wait, and release/recovery. OBSERVABILITY_STORE_LOCKED and OBSERVABILITY_STORE_UNLOCK_FAILED stop the operation; retain the original store and evidence. | [`kubeclaw.review:repository-audit`](../extend/plugin-catalogue/kubeclaw.review.md#stage-repository-audit) | Local library js-tiktoken@1.0.21, selected by the workspace package lock. ## Canonical Plugin Lifecycle Procedure
<!-- operator-task: plugin-lifecycle -->

### Supported start state, version, location, and authority

Start with a clean checkout at the exact source commit, an existing
`pipeline-platform.v2` file, an operator-supplied project or graph that uses the
plugin, and writable durable storage. Run commands from `<repository-root>` on
an administration machine with Node.js 24. The platform configuration owner,
not project content, controls installation roots, built-in trust, external
digests or attestations, providers, grants, adapters, observers, and storage.

The registry supports discovery and activation of packages already present in
approved installation roots. Internal APIs can copy or remove a local external
package, but the repository exposes no general operator CLI for those actions.
Do not turn an internal API or a filesystem copy into an undocumented operator
command.

### Preconditions

- Record the source commit and SHA-256 digest of `<platform.json>`.
- Identify exact package ID, registration ID, contract version, content digest,
  capability grants, dependencies, secret names, and every active run that pins
  the package.
- Complete the per-plugin external dependency matrix above and assign an owner to
  every required reachability check and failure response.
- Keep plugin code and schemas available for every nonterminal pinned run.
- Keep secret values only in the configured secret authority. Platform and
  project JSON may name secrets but must not contain their values.
- Prepare a controlled project or graph whose external effects are safe and
  whose success proves the selected registration.

Stop when a package identity is ambiguous, its digest or attestation is absent,
a dependency has no owner, a requested grant is wider than intended, or a
procedure would place a secret in evidence.

### 1. Discover and select

First complete the canonical
[Locked Dependency Installation](quickstart.md#locked-dependency-installation)
in the disposable checkout. Its retained evidence is part of this procedure's
evidence.

```bash
git status --short
git rev-parse HEAD
npm run plugin-system:inventory:check
npm run verify:plugin-packages
sha256sum "<platform.json>"
```

Expected observation: the worktree has no unexpected change, inventory and
package verification exit zero, and the recorded platform digest matches the
file to be used. Inspect the dynamic catalogue and select by exact IDs and
contract version, never by discovery order.

Check these platform fields together:

| Field | Operator decision |
| --- | --- |
| `installationRoots` | Canonical roots allowed for discovery |
| `trustedBuiltinRoots` | Which discovered roots are trusted built-ins |
| `externalTrust` | Exact source digest or verified-attestation binding |
| `providers` | One registration selected for each required capability |
| `grants` | The least capability scope for each registration |
| `adapters` and `activeAdapters` | Configuration and which adapters start |
| `observers` | Configuration for enabled observers |
| `storageRoot` | Persistent private run storage |

Relative roots resolve from the platform file, not the shell directory.

### 2. Validate a candidate configuration without starting work

Have the platform owner create `<candidate-platform.json>`. Then compile a
controlled project to a new output path:

```bash
test ! -e "<compiled-pipeline.json>"
npm run pipeline -- \
  --platform "<candidate-platform.json>" \
  --project "<controlled-project.json>" \
  --compile "<compiled-pipeline.json>"
sha256sum "<candidate-platform.json>" "<compiled-pipeline.json>"
```

Expected output is a JSON object with `status` equal to `compiled`, a run ID,
stage count, and definition digest. Compilation validates discovery, trust,
registrations, selected providers, schemas, configuration, and grants. It does
not prove an external endpoint, account, credential, or live dependency.

Distinguish failures before changing anything:

| Observation | Cause class | Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Manifest/schema/digest error | Package or trust input | Reject the candidate package |
| Duplicate ID or provider ambiguity | Registry selection | Select one exact registration; do not depend on order |
| Missing grant or denied resource | Authority policy | Confirm intended need; do not widen the grant merely to pass |
| Missing secret name | Secret configuration | Provision through its owner, then retry validation |
| Registration import error | Enabled module or isolation admission | Reject the candidate; inspect the named module and its import audit |
| Project requests uninstalled stage | Project/package mismatch | Correct the project or select an approved package |

### 3. Activate and prove health

Compilation imports enabled registration modules and checks their configuration,
trust, and grants. It does not call adapter factories or adapter `ready()`, create
a run, or prove a dependency. Treat compilation as code admission: approved plugin
modules are loaded even though no pipeline work starts.

A run then starts adapters in dependency order and awaits each adapter's
`ready()` result. The host races each factory and readiness call against
`shutdownTimeoutMs`. A readiness failure first revokes the failing adapter's
context and awaits its shutdown. Only after that cleanup settles can the outer
startup rollback revoke the other started contexts and attempt their shutdown.
No partially started adapter set becomes the usable runtime.
Failed-readiness teardown and startup rollback abort their shutdown signal after
`shutdownTimeoutMs`, but still await the adapter's shutdown promise. An adapter
that ignores the signal can therefore leave run startup pending indefinitely.
Pending failed-readiness cleanup also delays the outer rollback, so the earlier
adapters' contexts have not yet been revoked by that rollback.
See [adapter startup and shutdown limits](../understand/plugin-runtime.md#activation-is-fail-closed).
This startup transaction cannot reverse an external effect already accepted by
a service. After compile succeeds, start the controlled project only during its
approved window:

```bash
npm run pipeline -- \
  --platform "<candidate-platform.json>" \
  --project "<controlled-project.json>"
npm run pipeline -- \
  --platform "<candidate-platform.json>" \
  --audit "<run-id>"
```

Take `<run-id>` from the first command when it returns one. Stop new admission on
adapter startup or readiness failure, or when startup remains pending after its
timeout. Retain the adapter ID, dependency, configuration digest, elapsed time,
available run identity, and known or unresolved shutdown outcome. Do not treat
the timeout or a process stop as proof that cleanup completed. The plugin-runtime
maintainers own a stuck startup; the adapter owner must establish cleanup and
remaining resource ownership. Restore the dependency through its owner and
reconcile any uncertain external effect before retry. A process start
proves only that the process exists; adapter readiness proves its declared
startup checks; the controlled project's terminal result proves the selected
business operation.

Expected observation: the selected
registration appears in the resolved graph and attempt evidence, its functional
result succeeds, and audit state agrees with the terminal command output.
Registry activation alone is not health; the controlled functional result is
the health proof.

For endpoint, account, or data failures, use the
[canonical symptom diagnosis procedure](diagnose.md#canonical-symptom-diagnosis-procedure).
Do not retry an accepted external effect until its request and receipt are
reconciled.

### 4. Drain and disable

Stop admission of new projects that select the registration. Audit every active
run and let safe work reach a terminal state:

```bash
npm run pipeline -- --platform "<active-platform.json>" --audit "<run-id>"
```

There is no supported operator cancel command. If a run remains active or
waiting, keep the plugin package, schema, configuration, secrets, and dependency
available. Urgent process containment does not make the durable run cancelled.

After every pinned run is terminal, have the platform owner create a candidate
that removes the registration from provider selection, active adapters,
observers, and grants. Repeat the compile procedure. Expected observation: the
replacement controlled project compiles without the disabled registration and
unrelated registrations retain the same identities.

### 5. Replace or remove

For replacement, add and validate the new exact package before disabling the old
one. Prove the new functional path, drain old pinned runs, then remove old
selection and grants. Do not delete the old package while a run snapshot or
validator can still reference it.

For external package removal, stop after drain unless an approved operator tool
calls the owned removal API with authorization and a direct child of the
installation root. The repository currently supplies no such general command.
Manual filesystem deletion is unsupported. Built-in packages remain part of the
selected source tree; remove their activation from platform configuration, not
their files from a live checkout.

### Recovery, cleanup, and evidence

Before activation, recovery is to discard the candidate configuration. After a
new run begins, restore the previous compatible platform configuration only for
new admission; existing runs remain bound to their original graph and package
snapshot. Reconcile uncertain effects before another run.

Remove unused temporary compiled files and candidate files only after retaining
their hashes and final decision. Revoke a plugin secret only after all consumers
are drained and the old identity is proved unused. Retain source commit,
catalogue identity, package and platform digests, grants, dependency owners,
secret names, compile output, functional result, audit, drain inventory,
replacement proof, cleanup result, and exact blocked step.

## Source Authority

The platform schema defines operator-owned [installation roots, trust, providers,
and grants](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/config/platform.schema.json#L5-L54)
and [adapters, observers, and storage](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/config/platform.schema.json#L55-L94). Activation
[rechecks package integrity and loads only enabled registrations](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/registry/activation.ts#L103-L137).
External package code [validates policy, digest, manifest, limits, and modules](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/packages/install.ts#L154-L177)
before [atomically publishing the package](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/packages/install.ts#L180-L218),
while removal accepts only a direct child of the installation root
([implementation](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/common/plugin-runtime/foundation/packages/install.ts#L220-L234)).

Compilation [prepares and validates the admitted registry](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/nova/core/execution/engine.ts#L18-L22), including [enabled module imports](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/c8987b18b450bc27571d5037cb6ce3fb26e0cbd0/skills/nova/core/execution/engine-runtime.ts#L29-L41). Run startup [publishes the adapter set only after startup succeeds](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/7c85236b9a1992466ceb33b64f657062be57b4dd/skills/nova/core/execution/adapters.ts#L43-L49). The starter [orders dependencies, calls factories and readiness, and attempts rollback on failure](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/7c85236b9a1992466ceb33b64f657062be57b4dd/skills/nova/core/execution/adapter-startup.ts#L25-L61). Its [factory and readiness timeout race](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/7c85236b9a1992466ceb33b64f657062be57b4dd/skills/nova/core/execution/adapter-support.ts#L15-L18) does not bound the awaited [failed-readiness teardown and startup rollback](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/7c85236b9a1992466ceb33b64f657062be57b4dd/skills/nova/core/execution/adapter-startup.ts#L144-L165). Live external plugin activation has no verified outcome for this procedure.
